What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because it’s gotten hard to just ignore what’s happening at work. I’m a translator—mostly project files, translation memory, all that—and for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, they keep making these tiny changes… little things in the translation memory, timestamps shifting just enough, so later it looks like my quality control missed something.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds, but I’ve worked with these systems for years. It’s not like I’m guessing. IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t settle it for me, because if the record is arranged properly, of course it looks clean. It feels like the blame is being set up to land on me.\n\nSorry, I’m going in circles. It’s just—I used to enjoy this work, the precision of it, you know, when one word changes the whole meaning. Now I open a file and I’m checking not only the translation, but who touched it, when, and why. That’s basically why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these changes are being made intentionally to blame you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think it’s intentional. It’s the same pattern over and over in the project files, not just one mistake. Certain subcontractors, and there’s one colleague especially, they make these tiny changes to the translation memory or the timestamps, and later it makes it look like my quality control missed something.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that’s exactly the thing—if someone arranges the record carefully enough, then of course it looks clean. I’ve worked with these systems for years. It’s like when you’re translating one legal phrase and a single comma changes the whole responsibility in the sentence… people think that’s minor, but it’s not. That’s what this feels like. Small changes, but aimed so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these changes or is it just you who is aware of them?\n\nPatient: I think other people could notice if they looked properly, but most of them don’t want to look. Or they just accept what IT says, that the audit trail is clean, and then that becomes the end of it.\n\nFor me it’s obvious because I’m in those files every day—the translation memory, the timestamps, the little shifts. It’s like… when I was doing this one technical manual years ago, one repeated term kept changing depending on who had touched the file, and nobody cared until the client complained. So I learned to pay attention to small things.\n\nBut at work now, yes, I seem to be the one actually putting it together. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague especially, keep making these tiny changes so later it looks like my quality control failed. And if the record has been arranged carefully, then of course other people say, “No, nothing’s wrong.” That’s what worries me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these subcontractors and your colleague are working together against you?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in some way they must be. I don’t know if they’re all sitting in a room planning it, you know, but the same subcontractors and this one colleague keep showing up around the same kinds of changes. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that shift just enough, and then later it looks like I was the one who missed something in quality control.\n\nIt’s like when you’re checking a glossary and one term is off by just a little—most people skim right past it, but if you do this work long enough you know it changes the whole meaning. That’s what bothers me. IT says the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really reassure me, because if the record is arranged properly, of course it looks clean.\n\nSo, yes… I think they’re coordinating somehow to make the blame land on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes or stressors in your personal life recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it personal life exactly. Nothing dramatic comes to mind, if that’s what you mean. Work has been the main thing taking up space in my head.\n\nAt home it’s been… normal, mostly. I mean, there are always small things, errands, bills, family messages I forget to answer because I’m checking a file or thinking through wording from a job. Translating does that to you—one phrase gets stuck in your head and then you’re making coffee and still comparing two versions of a sentence.\n\nBut no, I don’t think there’s been some big personal event recently. Not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt someone was intentionally working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that way—“someone working against me” sounds very dramatic. In translation work you’re always dealing with other people’s changes, and sometimes a tiny change in a term or a timestamp matters more than people think. Years ago I had a glossary issue where everyone said it was nothing until it became a client problem, so I learned not to ignore patterns.\n\nBut this particular thing, no, not like this before. For months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files. The same subcontractors, and one colleague especially, keep being connected to these small changes in the translation memory and the timestamps. Then later it looks as if my quality control failed.\n\nAnd IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t settle it for me, because if the record has been arranged properly, it would look clean. That’s the part I can’t get past—how neatly it all ends up pointing back at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone was out to get you before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: Before this? Not really in this same way. I mean, people can be difficult at work, and in translation there are always revisions and second-guessing, so you learn to be careful.\n\nBut this feeling really started after I was put in charge of that new localization project. It’s been… about 11 months now, I think. Around then the little things started standing out more, and I began noticing how they kept circling back to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt like people were plotting against you, before you started noticing these changes at work?\n\nPatient: Before that, no, I don’t think so. Not in that “plotting” way. I’ve always been careful with work—when you’re translating, little wording choices can make people question everything—but this started after I was put in charge of the new localization project.\n\nIt’s been about 11 months, I think. Maybe shortly after that project began. That’s when I started noticing the changes at work more and feeling like they weren’t random.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are plotting against you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can get annoyed with people outside work like anyone does—family schedules, bills, someone forgetting to return a call—but I don’t sit there thinking they’re plotting against me.\n\nI still do my normal things. I translate, I keep the team workflow moving, pay bills, keep up with family routines. It’s when the project files come up, or the file history, or certain coworkers’ names attached to changes… that’s when I start turning it over and over. Outside of that, things are mostly normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are plotting against you when you're not thinking about work or the issues at your job?\n\nPatient: No, not really when I’m not thinking about work. I mean, life is still life — I’m still translating, keeping the team’s workflow moving, paying bills, dealing with family routines and all that. Like, my son had this school thing last week and I was focused on getting everyone out the door, not thinking about coworkers.\n\nIt’s really when the project comes up — the file history, who touched what, certain names attached to changes — that’s when my mind starts going off track and I start connecting things. Outside of that, I function pretty normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are plotting against you when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: No, not if I’m really not thinking about work. It keeps coming back to the files, you know? The translation memory, the timestamps, who made which tiny change. That’s where the pattern is.\n\nI know IT says the audit trail is clean, but… that doesn’t convince me. It feels arranged, like certain subcontractors and one colleague are making it look like my quality control failed. Outside of that, if I’m making dinner or dealing with something at home, I’m not usually thinking people are plotting. It’s this project record that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that people are plotting against you when you're completely relaxed and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Completely relaxed… I don’t know, that’s harder to separate, because my mind sort of drifts back to the same thing. Like if I’m sitting there quietly, I might suddenly think about the project files again.\n\nIt’s not “people” in general. It’s that same pattern I’ve been seeing for months — certain subcontractors, and that one colleague, making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, so later it looks like my quality control failed. And IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. The record can look clean and still be arranged, you know?\n\nSorry, I know you asked about when I’m relaxed. If I’m truly not thinking about anything, then no, not usually. But it doesn’t take much for my mind to go back to those files.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people outside of work are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of work, not really. I mean, outside that project issue I’m still doing normal things — translating, keeping the team’s workflow moving, paying bills, taking care of family routines.\n\nLike yesterday I was just trying to sort out a bill and then my kid needed something, and that’s where my attention was. It’s not like I think the neighbors or people at the store are plotting. The place it goes off track is when the file history comes up, or certain coworkers’ names, or who changed what. That’s when I start… you know, seeing the pattern again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are plotting against you when you are at home with your family?\n\nPatient: At home with my family? No, not in the sense that I think my family is involved or anything like that. A record can look clean and still be arranged so the blame lands on me. So… different environment, yes, I can step away from it a little. Outside of work? No, not in that way. I don’t think my neighbor or the cashier at the store is involved or anything like that.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sure, I have moments. Like if I’m watching something with my wife or, you know, trying to fix the sink—actually the sink has been dripping for weeks, which is its own little project—but then my mind circles back.

It’s not that I think everyone everywhere is plotting. It’s more… I’m here because for months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, and then later it looks like my quality control failed.

So even if I have a break from it for a bit, once I remember the files, it’s right there again. And IT can say the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t reassure me. A record can be arranged to look clean. That’s how the blame gets put on me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because it’s gotten hard to just ignore what’s happening at work. I’m a translator—mostly project files, translation memory, all that—and for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, they keep making these tiny changes… little things in the translation memory, timestamps shifting just enough, so later it looks like my quality control missed something.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds, but I’ve worked with these systems for years. It’s not like I’m guessing. IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t settle it for me, because if the record is arranged properly, of course it looks clean. It feels like the blame is being set up to land on me.\n\nSorry, I’m going in circles. It’s just—I used to enjoy this work, the precision of it, you know, when one word changes the whole meaning. Now I open a file and I’m checking not only the translation, but who touched it, when, and why. That’s basically why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these changes are being made intentionally to blame you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think it’s intentional. It’s the same pattern over and over in the project files, not just one mistake. Certain subcontractors, and there’s one colleague especially, they make these tiny changes to the translation memory or the timestamps, and later it makes it look like my quality control missed something.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that’s exactly the thing—if someone arranges the record carefully enough, then of course it looks clean. I’ve worked with these systems for years. It’s like when you’re translating one legal phrase and a single comma changes the whole responsibility in the sentence… people think that’s minor, but it’s not. That’s what this feels like. Small changes, but aimed so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these changes or is it just you who is aware of them?\n\nPatient: I think other people could notice if they looked properly, but most of them don’t want to look. Or they just accept what IT says, that the audit trail is clean, and then that becomes the end of it.\n\nFor me it’s obvious because I’m in those files every day—the translation memory, the timestamps, the little shifts. It’s like… when I was doing this one technical manual years ago, one repeated term kept changing depending on who had touched the file, and nobody cared until the client complained. So I learned to pay attention to small things.\n\nBut at work now, yes, I seem to be the one actually putting it together. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague especially, keep making these tiny changes so later it looks like my quality control failed. And if the record has been arranged carefully, then of course other people say, “No, nothing’s wrong.” That’s what worries me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these subcontractors and your colleague are working together against you?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in some way they must be. I don’t know if they’re all sitting in a room planning it, you know, but the same subcontractors and this one colleague keep showing up around the same kinds of changes. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that shift just enough, and then later it looks like I was the one who missed something in quality control.\n\nIt’s like when you’re checking a glossary and one term is off by just a little—most people skim right past it, but if you do this work long enough you know it changes the whole meaning. That’s what bothers me. IT says the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really reassure me, because if the record is arranged properly, of course it looks clean.\n\nSo, yes… I think they’re coordinating somehow to make the blame land on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes or stressors in your personal life recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it personal life exactly. Nothing dramatic comes to mind, if that’s what you mean. Work has been the main thing taking up space in my head.\n\nAt home it’s been… normal, mostly. I mean, there are always small things, errands, bills, family messages I forget to answer because I’m checking a file or thinking through wording from a job. Translating does that to you—one phrase gets stuck in your head and then you’re making coffee and still comparing two versions of a sentence.\n\nBut no, I don’t think there’s been some big personal event recently. Not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt someone was intentionally working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that way—“someone working against me” sounds very dramatic. In translation work you’re always dealing with other people’s changes, and sometimes a tiny change in a term or a timestamp matters more than people think. Years ago I had a glossary issue where everyone said it was nothing until it became a client problem, so I learned not to ignore patterns.\n\nBut this particular thing, no, not like this before. For months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files. The same subcontractors, and one colleague especially, keep being connected to these small changes in the translation memory and the timestamps. Then later it looks as if my quality control failed.\n\nAnd IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t settle it for me, because if the record has been arranged properly, it would look clean. That’s the part I can’t get past—how neatly it all ends up pointing back at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone was out to get you before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: Before this? Not really in this same way. I mean, people can be difficult at work, and in translation there are always revisions and second-guessing, so you learn to be careful.\n\nBut this feeling really started after I was put in charge of that new localization project. It’s been… about 11 months now, I think. Around then the little things started standing out more, and I began noticing how they kept circling back to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt like people were plotting against you, before you started noticing these changes at work?\n\nPatient: Before that, no, I don’t think so. Not in that “plotting” way. I’ve always been careful with work—when you’re translating, little wording choices can make people question everything—but this started after I was put in charge of the new localization project.\n\nIt’s been about 11 months, I think. Maybe shortly after that project began. That’s when I started noticing the changes at work more and feeling like they weren’t random.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are plotting against you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can get annoyed with people outside work like anyone does—family schedules, bills, someone forgetting to return a call—but I don’t sit there thinking they’re plotting against me.\n\nI still do my normal things. I translate, I keep the team workflow moving, pay bills, keep up with family routines. It’s when the project files come up, or the file history, or certain coworkers’ names attached to changes… that’s when I start turning it over and over. Outside of that, things are mostly normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are plotting against you when you're not thinking about work or the issues at your job?\n\nPatient: No, not really when I’m not thinking about work. I mean, life is still life — I’m still translating, keeping the team’s workflow moving, paying bills, dealing with family routines and all that. Like, my son had this school thing last week and I was focused on getting everyone out the door, not thinking about coworkers.\n\nIt’s really when the project comes up — the file history, who touched what, certain names attached to changes — that’s when my mind starts going off track and I start connecting things. Outside of that, I function pretty normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are plotting against you when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: No, not if I’m really not thinking about work. It keeps coming back to the files, you know? The translation memory, the timestamps, who made which tiny change. That’s where the pattern is.\n\nI know IT says the audit trail is clean, but… that doesn’t convince me. It feels arranged, like certain subcontractors and one colleague are making it look like my quality control failed. Outside of that, if I’m making dinner or dealing with something at home, I’m not usually thinking people are plotting. It’s this project record that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that people are plotting against you when you're completely relaxed and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Completely relaxed… I don’t know, that’s harder to separate, because my mind sort of drifts back to the same thing. Like if I’m sitting there quietly, I might suddenly think about the project files again.\n\nIt’s not “people” in general. It’s that same pattern I’ve been seeing for months — certain subcontractors, and that one colleague, making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, so later it looks like my quality control failed. And IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. The record can look clean and still be arranged, you know?\n\nSorry, I know you asked about when I’m relaxed. If I’m truly not thinking about anything, then no, not usually. But it doesn’t take much for my mind to go back to those files.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people outside of work are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of work, not really. I mean, outside that project issue I’m still doing normal things — translating, keeping the team’s workflow moving, paying bills, taking care of family routines.\n\nLike yesterday I was just trying to sort out a bill and then my kid needed something, and that’s where my attention was. It’s not like I think the neighbors or people at the store are plotting. The place it goes off track is when the file history comes up, or certain coworkers’ names, or who changed what. That’s when I start… you know, seeing the pattern again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are plotting against you when you are at home with your family?\n\nPatient: At home with my family? No, not in the sense that I think my family is involved or anything like that. If I’m helping with dinner or my kid is asking me something, it’s mostly normal.\n\nBut then, you know, I’ll be sitting there and some detail from work comes back—like the translation memory, or who touched the file last, or those timestamps. And then I’m right back to it. Because for months it’s been the same pattern: certain subcontractors and one colleague making these tiny changes so it looks like my quality control failed. IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but I don’t buy that. A clean-looking record can still be arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nSorry, I know you asked about home. At home, no, not generally. It’s when my mind goes back to those project files.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that people are plotting against you when you're in a completely different environment, like on vacation or visiting friends?\n\nPatient: If I’m visiting friends or away somewhere, it’s not like I suddenly think random people there are plotting. I can usually be distracted by the place, or the conversation, or, you know, what we’re eating, where we’re going—my wife always likes to plan those things down to the minute.\n\nBut it can still come back to me. Like if there’s a quiet moment, or I check my email, or someone mentions work, then I start thinking about the same project files again. That’s why I’m here, really. For months I’ve been seeing this pattern with certain subcontractors and one colleague—tiny changes to the translation memory, timestamps not lining up in a way that feels innocent—and then later it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nIT says the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t reassure me much. A record can look clean and still be arranged so the blame lands on me. So… different environment, yes, I can step away from it a little. But the concern doesn’t just disappear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are plotting against you when you're not thinking about work at all?\n\nPatient: When I’m truly not thinking about work? No, not really. If I’m focused on, I don’t know, grocery shopping or helping at home, I’m not thinking people in general are plotting.\n\nBut the thing is, it doesn’t stay separate for long. I’ll be doing something completely ordinary—like yesterday I was trying to find the right charger, and then I remembered I had left an email unanswered, and then suddenly I’m back with the project files in my head. Because that’s the issue. For months I’ve been seeing the same pattern: certain subcontractors and one colleague making these tiny changes in the translation memory, the timestamps, little things that later make it look like my quality control failed.\n\nAnd IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t convince me. A record can be made to look clean. That’s sort of the point. It’s arranged so the blame lands on me. So if I’m not thinking about work at all, no, but once that thread gets pulled, it’s right there again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a day where you don't think about people plotting against you at work?\n\nPatient: A whole day? I mean… maybe there are parts of days where I don’t think about it. If I’m busy with my family or running errands, it can fade into the background for a while.\n\nBut no, lately I don’t think I get through a full day without it coming back. It’s been months of seeing the same thing in those project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, tiny changes that certain subcontractors and one colleague keep making. And then somehow it makes my quality control look like the problem.\n\nI know IT says the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Records can be arranged to look clean. That’s what bothers me. It feels set up so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people outside of your work are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? No, not in that way. I don’t think my neighbor or the cashier at the store is involved or anything like that.\n\nIt’s really tied to work. I mean, I’ll admit I can carry it around with me—like I’ll be making dinner and then remember some file timestamp, and it’s there again. My wife says I get stuck on it, and maybe I do. But I’m here because for months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nAnd IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t prove much to me. Records can be made to look clean. That’s what I keep trying to explain. It’s arranged so the blame ends up on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a moment, regardless of where you are or what you're doing, when you don't think someone is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Sure, I have moments. Like if I’m watching something with my wife or, you know, trying to fix the sink—actually the sink has been dripping for weeks, which is its own little project—but then my mind circles back.\n\nIt’s not that I think everyone everywhere is plotting. It’s more… I’m here because for months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, and then later it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nSo even if I have a break from it for a bit, once I remember the files, it’s right there again. Completely forget? Sometimes for a short stretch, yeah, if I’m absorbed in something else. Like yesterday I was trying to compare prices on dishwasher parts, and then I got sidetracked reading reviews—people get very intense about appliances—but even then it sort of comes back.

Because I’m here because this has been going on for months. I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, and then it ends up looking like my quality control failed.

So maybe I forget for a little while, but not for long. And IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but that doesn’t really convince me. A clean record can be arranged that way. That’s the whole point—it puts the blame on me.
